Explanations:
      1. Dynamic Margin Rates (Professional Client)
         The Dynamic Margin Rates mechanism is applied exclusively to Professional Clients and pertains to those instruments for which multi-level margin rates are defined in the Dynamic Margin Rates (Professional Client) column of the specification.
         These rates depend on the total nominal value (exposure) of all open positions on a given instrument, expressed in USD. The rates are applied on a tiered basis.
         The key principle is that when an exposure threshold for a given tier is exceeded, the new, higher margin rate applies only to the surplus over that threshold.

        Example of Dynamic Margin Rates Application
        Let's assume a Professional Client is trading the EURUSD.pro instrument, for which the margin tiers are as follows:
                   Tier 1 (up to $3M): 0.50% rate
                   Tier 2 (from $3M to $10M): 1.00% rate
        Scenario: Exposure Exceeding a Threshold
        The client opens positions, and their total nominal value (exposure) reaches $5,000,000.
        Margin Calculation:
        The margin is calculated separately for each portion of the exposure that falls within a given tier:
                a. Portion in Tier 1:
                              Exposure: $3,000,000
                              Rate: 0.50%
                              Margin for this portion: $3,000,000 * 0.50% = $15,000
                b. Portion in Tier 2 (surplus over $3M):
                              Exposure: $5,000,000 - $3,000,000 = $2,000,000
                              Rate: 1.00%
                              Margin for this portion: $2,000,000 * 1.00% = $20,000
        Total required margin:
        $15,000 (from Tier 1) + $20,000 (from Tier 2) = $35,000
     2. Minimum quotation step - this is the minimum value by which the price of the quoted Financial Instruments can change.
     3. Lot - is the transaction unit for all Financial Instruments.
     4. Nominal value - indicates the nominal size of the opened position; this size is necessary to calculate the required margin, and all charges included in the Table of Fees and Commissions are made in reference to the Nominal Value.
     5. Trading hours for individual Financial Instruments from the last Sunday of October to the last Saturday of March are expressed in Central European Time (CET), while from the last Sunday of March to the last Saturday of October, they are expressed in Summer Time
        (CEST).
     6. Maximum order size for individual Financial Instruments is an indicative value and may change in case of significant volatility or limited liquidity of the Underlying Instrument; detailed conditions are specified in the Regulations. Current order sizes are defined in the
        Transaction System.
     7. All Financial Instruments are quoted in a system of variable spreads, the value of which depends on the current market situation, with particular emphasis on volatility, liquidity, and market depth. The quoting of instruments reflects the price received from the Quoting
        Source, to which an additional markup may be applied, not greater than that specified in the Table of Fees and Commissions.
     8. Any changes to the Position Security Level (leverage) apply to both newly opened and previously held positions on the account.
     9. In the case of CFDs on stocks, the availability of short selling may be limited for a given security depending on market conditions. Changes in the availability of short selling may occur immediately.
    10. In the event that three margin rates are provided for a given financial instrument in the "Margin Rates" column, the first and second rates apply to an Account to which the rules for a Retail Client and an Experienced Retail Client apply, respectively.
    11. In the case of CFDs quoted based on futures contracts, the series of the futures contract is changed over time. If the Client maintains a position in such a CFD after the change of a given futures contract series, the result will be adjusted by the value of swap points
        resulting from the difference between the price of the expiring series and the price of the new series. The value of swap points charged during the rollover of a CFD based on a futures contract will be adjusted by the amount of the spread (maximum) or by a smaller
        value than resulting from the spread on the given instrument.
        Example:
        On the OILWTI instrument, the underlying contracts are rolled over from the May series CLK6 to the June series CLM6. The basis between the contracts is 100 points, where the June series is quoted higher than the May series (the market is in contango). Long positions
        will be adjusted downwards by a value equal to 102.5 points (=100+half of the spread on OILWTI), while short positions will be adjusted upwards by a value equal to 97.5 points (=100-half of the spread on OILWTI).
    12. The quoted price in the transaction system cannot show a negative value. This also means that you cannot place pending orders with a negative price. If the underlying instrument, which is an instrument traded on an organized market, reaches a negative price on
        that organized market, OANDA TMS Brokers S.A. will halt trading on the given financial instrument until further notice and will simultaneously close all open positions on the Client's account at the last non-negative price available in the transaction system.
    13. "Limit" type orders are executed during the following hours:
                    9:00 - 17:30: CFDs on stocks (German, Spanish, French, Dutch, Belgian, Portuguese, British).
                    9:00 - 16:55: CFDs on Danish stocks.
                    9:00 - 17:25: CFDs on Swedish, Finnish stocks.
                    9:00 - 16:50: CFDs on Polish stocks.
                    15:30 - 22:00: CFDs on US stocks, ETFs.

    14. British stocks are quoted in pence (GBX). 1 GBP = 100 GBX.
    15. Instruments EURTRY.pro; USDTRY.pro are quoted in "close only" mode until further notice.
